S. d. § 10 EStG ist, dass sie nicht in unmittelbarem wirtschaftlichen Zusammenhang mit steuerfreien Einnahmen stehen. Dieser Zusammenhang ist z. B. in folgenden Fällen gegeben:<br>Gesetzliche Arbeitnehmeranteile, die auf steuerfreien Arbeitslohn entfallen (z. B. nach dem Auslandstätigkeitserlass, aufgrund eines DBA oder aufgrund des zusätzlichen Höchstbetrags von 1.800 € nach § 3 Nummer 63 Satz 3 EStG),<br>Aufwendungen aus Mitteln, die nach ihrer Zweckbestimmung zur Leistung der Vorsorgeaufwendungen dienen:<br>Beiträge an die schweizerische Alters‑ und Hinterlassenenversicherung können nicht bei der Ermittlung des besonderen Steuersatzes im Rahmen des Progressionsvorbehaltes berücksichtigt werden (>BFH vom 18.04.2012 – X R 62/09, BStBl II S. 721). | 2026-10-29 |

| 184 | Example 2: arrival on 1 July 2026 with foreign pay for January to June | Clara moves from London to Munich on 1 July 2026 and takes a job there. January to June she earned EUR 30,000 (converted) from her UK employer; she had no German income in those months. July to December she earns EUR 36,000 gross in Germany. Her Werbungskosten are below the lump sum. Deductible insurance for her German months, from the certificates: EUR 5,500. Single, no church, no other income. | 220 | Ask the client first | 1. **Partners:** the partnership agreement's profit-sharing key, any advance shares, and every payment the partnership made to each partner (pay, interest, rent). This decides the share, the Sondervergütungen and the § 35 split. | — | intake question; positive parameters sourced in current substantive rows | — |
| 221 | Ask the client first | 2. **Partners:** what the partnership does and who manages it. A commercial side line or a GmbH as sole liable partner makes the whole partnership commercial and liable to trade tax. | — | intake question; positive parameters sourced in current substantive rows | — |
| 222 | Ask the client first | 3. **Limited partners with a loss:** the capital account at year end, the registered liability and the contribution actually paid (§ 15a). | — | intake question; positive parameters sourced in current substantive rows | — |
| 223 | Ask the client first | 4. **Year of moving:** the exact date the German home was taken up or given up, and where the person lived before or after. | — | intake question; positive parameters sourced in current substantive rows | — |
| 224 | Ask the client first | 5. **Year of moving:** all foreign income of the other months, by type and amount, and any foreign income in the resident months that a treaty exempts (and where the property or establishment is). | — | intake question; positive parameters sourced in current substantive rows | — |
| 225 | Ask the client first | 6. Married? Did the spouse move on the same date? | — | intake question; positive parameters sourced in current substantive rows | — |

Every actual old nonblank unit is retained below. Exact wording is distinguished from corrected law, historical examples and specific unverified assertions; no separately verified platform body is invented.

| b0e37140e138:15 | This skill covers two gaps the other Germany skills leave open. Part 1 is the partner in a partnership: how the partnership's profit is fixed once for all partners and then taxed in each partner's own return. Part 2 is the person who moves to or from Germany during 2026: one assessment as a resident for the whole year, with the foreign income of the other months raising the tax rate. | Exact wording retained at current LF18, Germany: partners' profit shares and the year of moving to or from Germany, tax year 2026 |
| b0e37140e138:17 | See also, and do not repeat here: | Exact wording retained at current LF20, Germany: partners' profit shares and the year of moving to or from Germany, tax year 2026 |
| b0e37140e138:19 | - **de-income-tax**: the 2026 tariff, special expenses, solidarity surcharge, church tax, the § 35 credit for a sole trader, deadlines and penalties for the partner's own return. | Exact wording retained at current LF22, Germany: partners' profit shares and the year of moving to or from Germany, tax year 2026 |
| b0e37140e138:20 | - **de-trade-tax**: trade tax at partnership level (add-backs, reductions, the EUR 24,500 allowance, multiplier), Abfärbung and gewerbliche Prägung in detail. | Exact wording retained at current LF23, Germany: partners' profit shares and the year of moving to or from Germany, tax year 2026 |
| b0e37140e138:21 | - **de-employee-income-tax**: wages, Werbungskosten, joint assessment, Anlage N-AUS. | Exact wording retained at current LF24, Germany: partners' profit shares and the year of moving to or from Germany, tax year 2026 |
| b0e37140e138:22 | - **de-non-resident-tax**: limited liability, German-source income (§ 49 EStG), the § 1(3) option, foreign tax credits (§ 34c EStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF25, Germany: partners' profit shares and the year of moving to or from Germany, tax year 2026 |
| b0e37140e138:23 | - **leaving-germany-exit-tax**: § 6 AStG on shares and the extended limited liability after a move. | Exact wording retained at current LF26, Germany: partners' profit shares and the year of moving to or from Germany, tax year 2026 |
| b0e37140e138:27 | **Part 1, partners.** | Exact wording retained at current LF30, Who this applies to |
| b0e37140e138:29 | - **In scope.** A person resident in Germany (§ 1(1) EStG) who is a co-entrepreneur (Mitunternehmer) of a partnership: a partner in an OHG, a KG (general or limited partner), a GmbH & Co. KG, a GbR or a PartG that runs a business or a profession. Their income is "die Gewinnanteile der Gesellschafter ... und die Vergütungen, die der Gesellschafter von der Gesellschaft für seine Tätigkeit im Dienst der Gesellschaft oder für die Hingabe von Darlehen oder für die Überlassung von Wirtschaftsgütern bezogen hat" (§ 15(1) sentence 1 no. 2 EStG). A partner holding through another partnership is treated the same way (§ 15(1) sentence 1 no. 2 sentence 2). | Exact wording retained at current LF32, Who this applies to |
| b0e37140e138:30 | - **Trade or profession.** A partnership whose partners all practise a free profession (§ 18(1) no. 1 EStG: doctors, lawyers, engineers, architects, tax advisers and the like) has income from self-employed work; § 18(4) applies § 15(1) no. 2 and § 15a to it, so the same profit share and Sondervergütung rules apply. It pays no trade tax unless one of two rules makes the whole activity a trade: | Exact wording retained at current LF33, Who this applies to |
| b0e37140e138:31 |   - **Abfärbung (§ 15(3) no. 1 EStG).** Any commercial activity of its own, or commercial income from a share in another partnership, makes the whole activity commercial, whether that part makes a profit or a loss. | Exact wording retained at current LF34, Who this applies to |
| b0e37140e138:32 |   - **Gewerbliche Prägung (§ 15(3) no. 2 EStG).** A partnership with no commercial activity whose only personally liable partners are capital companies, managed only by them or by non-partners. A GmbH & Co. KG run by its GmbH is the usual case. | Exact wording retained at current LF35, Who this applies to |
| b0e37140e138:33 | - **Out of scope.** Partnerships that opted to be taxed as a company (§ 1a KStG); partners that are companies (de-corporate-tax); sale or closing of a partnership share (§§ 16, 34 EStG); a change of partners during the year; foreign partnerships. Refer. | Exact wording retained at current LF36, Who this applies to |
| b0e37140e138:35 | **Part 2, the year of moving.** | Exact wording retained at current LF38, Who this applies to |
| b0e37140e138:37 | - **In scope.** An individual who, in 2026, takes up or gives up a German home or habitual abode, so that they are fully liable for part of the year and either limited liable or not liable at all for the rest. Arrivals and departures alike. | Exact wording retained at current LF40, Who this applies to |
| b0e37140e138:38 | - **The rule.** Income tax is an annual tax, assessed per calendar year (§ 2(7) sentences 1 and 2 EStG). "Besteht während eines Kalenderjahres sowohl unbeschränkte als auch beschränkte Einkommensteuerpflicht, so sind die während der beschränkten Einkommensteuerpflicht erzielten inländischen Einkünfte in eine Veranlagung zur unbeschränkten Einkommensteuerpflicht einzubeziehen" (§ 2(7) sentence 3). So there is one assessment as a resident, covering the whole year. | Exact wording retained at current LF41, Who this applies to |
| b0e37140e138:39 | - **Out of scope.** The exit tax on shares when leaving (leaving-germany-exit-tax); a person who never becomes resident (de-non-resident-tax); the extended limited liability under § 2 AStG. | Exact wording retained at current LF42, Who this applies to |

| b0e37140e138:63 | **Step 1: the partnership's profit is fixed once.** Where several people share in income, it is fixed separately and uniformly for all of them (§ 180(1) sentence 1 no. 2 (a) AO; § 179(2) AO). The determination notice (Feststellungsbescheid) binds each partner's own assessment. It fixes: | Exact wording retained at current LF66, Part 1: partners |
| b0e37140e138:65 | 1. **The partnership's own profit** (Gesamthandsgewinn), from its books or cash accounts. Trade tax is not deductible (§ 4(5b) EStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF68, Part 1: partners |
| b0e37140e138:66 | 2. **Each partner's Sondervergütungen**: pay for work for the partnership, interest on loans to it, rent for assets let to it (§ 15(1) no. 2 EStG). If the partnership booked them as costs, they are added back to that partner. They are business income, not wages, interest or rent. | Exact wording retained at current LF69, Part 1: partners |
| b0e37140e138:67 | 3. **Each partner's special business income and costs** (Sonderbetriebseinnahmen and -ausgaben): for example interest on a loan the partner took to buy the share, or the partner's own costs on a building let to the partnership. Each partner reports these to the partnership so they go into the determination. | Exact wording retained at current LF70, Part 1: partners |
| b0e37140e138:68 | 4. **The split.** Profit share by the partnership agreement, plus the partner's own items from 2 and 3. | Exact wording retained at current LF71, Part 1: partners |
| b0e37140e138:69 | 5. **Other items**: income exempt under a treaty that matters for the partners' rate (§ 180(5) no. 1 AO), and tax withheld to be credited (§ 180(5) no. 2 AO); for a commercial partnership, the § 35 figures (Step 3). | Exact wording retained at current LF72, Part 1: partners |
| b0e37140e138:71 | **Step 2: the partner's own return.** The partner takes the determined share into their own return as business income (trade or self-employed), adds any other income, and from there follows de-income-tax: special expenses, tariff, surcharge, church tax. The partner does not recompute the share; if it is wrong, the determination notice is what gets challenged. | Exact wording retained at current LF74, Part 1: partners |
| b0e37140e138:73 | **Step 3: trade tax and the § 35 credit.** A commercial partnership pays trade tax itself (§ 5 GewStG; see de-trade-tax). Its trade income starts from the profit fixed in Step 1, Sondervergütungen and special items included (§ 7 GewStG). A partner's share of profit from another partnership is taken out again (§ 9 no. 2 GewStG), because that partnership pays its own trade tax. The base amount, the trade tax actually payable and each partner's share of both are fixed in the same determination (§ 35(2) sentence 1 and (4) EStG). Each partner's share follows the general profit-sharing key, ignoring advance shares (§ 35(2) sentence 2). The BMF letter on § 35 says the same for Sondervergütungen and for results of special and supplementary balance sheets (BMF 3 November 2016, Rn. 22), and a partner that cannot use the credit, such as a GmbH general partner, still takes its key share (Rn. 24). A managing partner's Sondervergütung therefore gives no extra credit. The partner's credit is the lowest of: 4 × share of the base amount; the tax on trade income in the partner's own return (positive trade income / all positive income × tariff tax, § 35(1) sentence 2); the share of trade tax actually payable. Unused credit is lost. | Exact wording retained at current LF76, Part 1: partners |
| b0e37140e138:75 | **Step 4: losses of limited partners (§ 15a EStG).** A limited partner's loss share cannot be set against other income, or carried back or forward under § 10d, so far as it creates or increases a negative capital account. Exception: losses up to the amount by which the registered liability exceeds the contribution actually paid, if the partner is entered in the commercial register, the liability is proven, and a claim under it is not excluded by contract or unlikely (§ 15a(1) sentences 2 and 3). The blocked loss is only set against later profits from the same partnership (§ 15a(2)). It is fixed each year in a separate notice (§ 15a(4)). Withdrawals that create or increase a negative capital account are added as profit, up to the losses that were deductible in that year and the ten years before (§ 15a(3)). The same rule applies to atypical silent partners and to GbR partners whose liability is excluded or unlikely (§ 15a(5)). | Exact wording retained at current LF78, Part 1: partners |
| b0e37140e138:77 | **Step 5: the retained-profits option (§ 34a EStG), in summary.** A partner whose profit is computed from balance sheets (§ 4(1) or § 5) and whose share is over 10% or over EUR 10,000 may apply, per partnership share and per year, for profit left in the business to be taxed at 28.25% in 2026 instead of the tariff. Profit left in = profit less the positive balance of withdrawals over contributions, plus trade tax (§ 34a(2)). When later withdrawals exceed later profits, the amount fixed as liable to recapture is taxed at 25% (§ 34a(4)). The surcharge applies to both. The calculation is not set out here: refer. | Exact wording retained at current LF80, Part 1: partners |
| b0e37140e138:81 | **Step 1: split the year.** Fix the date the German home or habitual abode began or ended. The months before arrival or after departure are the "other period". | Exact wording retained at current LF84, Part 2: the year of moving |
| b0e37140e138:83 | **Step 2: the taxable base.** It contains: | Exact wording retained at current LF86, Part 2: the year of moving |
| b0e37140e138:85 | - all income of the resident months, worldwide (§ 2(1) EStG: income earned "während seiner unbeschränkten Einkommensteuerpflicht"); | Exact wording retained at current LF88, Part 2: the year of moving |
| b0e37140e138:86 | - German-source income of the other period (§ 49 EStG; see de-non-resident-tax), for example rent from a German flat let before arrival (§ 2(7) sentence 3); | Exact wording retained at current LF89, Part 2: the year of moving |
| b0e37140e138:87 | - the full basic allowance and the full employee lump sum: there is no cut by months for a resident assessment. Special expenses of the resident months are deducted as usual; for contributions paid in the other months see Cross-border points. | Exact wording retained at current LF90, Part 2: the year of moving |
| b0e37140e138:89 | **Step 3: income that raises the rate.** These are left out of the base but added to find the rate (§ 32b(1) sentence 1): | Exact wording retained at current LF92, Part 2: the year of moving |
| b0e37140e138:91 | - **No. 2: foreign income of the other period** that was not subject to German tax ("ausländische Einkünfte, die im Veranlagungszeitraum nicht der deutschen Einkommensteuer unterlegen haben"), for cases of part-year full liability, including § 2(7) sentence 3 cases. The official guidance confirms: on arrival or departure, income earned outside the period of full liability counts for the progression clause if it is not subject to limited liability (EStH 2025, H 32b "Zeitweise unbeschränkte Steuerpflicht"). | Exact wording retained at current LF94, Part 2: the year of moving |
| b0e37140e138:92 | - **No. 3: income a tax treaty exempts in Germany**, in the resident months (often foreign employment income or foreign rent). | Exact wording retained at current LF95, Part 2: the year of moving |
| b0e37140e138:94 | **Exception for EU and EEA items under no. 3 only (§ 32b(1) sentence 2).** Treaty-exempt rent from property in another EU or EEA state, profits of an EU or EEA farm, and profits of an EU or EEA commercial establishment that fails the active-business test of § 2a(2), do not raise the rate. The exception is written for no. 3 only. Foreign rent of the months before arrival falls under no. 2 and raises the rate wherever the property is. | Exact wording retained at current LF97, Part 2: the year of moving |
| b0e37140e138:96 | **Step 4: the amount added (§ 32b(2)).** Foreign income is computed under German rules: profit or surplus, not gross receipts. For foreign pay, the EUR 1,230 employee lump sum is deducted only to the extent it was not already deducted against German pay, and actual Werbungskosten only so far as all Werbungskosten together exceed the lump sum (§ 32b(2) sentence 2 (a) and (b)). Extraordinary income in it counts at one fifth. Foreign losses lower the rate and can bring it to zero (EStH 2025, H 32b "Ausländische Verluste"). Contributions to the Swiss AHV do not reduce the amount added (same, citing BFH 18 April 2012). | Exact wording retained at current LF99, Part 2: the year of moving |
| b0e37140e138:98 | **Step 5: the tax.** Tariff tax on (taxable income + amount added), divided by that sum, cut to four decimal places of a percent = special rate. Special rate × taxable income, rounded down = income tax. Then the surcharge and church tax on that tax (de-income-tax). | Exact wording retained at current LF101, Part 2: the year of moving |
| b0e37140e138:100 | **Joint assessment in the year of moving.** Spouses may choose it if both were fully liable and not permanently separated at some time in the year (§ 26(1) EStG). If both move together, the year qualifies. | Exact wording retained at current LF103, Part 2: the year of moving |
| b0e37140e138:104 | \| Step \| Rule \| | Exact wording retained at current LF107, Deadlines and filing |
| b0e37140e138:106 | \| Partnership return \| Erklärung zur gesonderten und einheitlichen Feststellung (main form ESt 1 B, with Anlage FB for the partners and Anlage FE-G or FE-S for the income; names from the ELSTER 2025 guide). A partnership with legal capacity files it, and if it does not, each partner must; for one without legal capacity, each partner (§ 181(2) AO). Electronic only, unless the office allows paper for hardship (§ 181(2a) AO) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF109, Deadlines and filing |
| b0e37140e138:107 | \| Partnership return deadline, own preparation \| Seven months after year end (§ 149(2) AO, applied by § 181(1) AO): 31 July 2027 is a Saturday, so Monday 2 August 2027 (§ 108(3) AO) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF110, Deadlines and filing |
| b0e37140e138:108 | \| Partnership return deadline, tax adviser \| Last day of February of the second year after: 29 February 2028 (§ 149(3) no. 5 AO). The office can ask for it earlier (§ 149(4) AO) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF111, Deadlines and filing |
| b0e37140e138:109 | \| Trade tax return of a commercial partnership \| Same deadlines (§ 149(3) no. 3 AO); see de-trade-tax \| | Exact wording retained at current LF112, Deadlines and filing |
| b0e37140e138:110 | \| Partner's own return \| Deadlines in de-income-tax (2 August 2027, or 29 February 2028 with an adviser) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF113, Deadlines and filing |
| b0e37140e138:111 | \| Changes flow through \| A change to the determination changes the partner's assessment; a change to profit changes the trade tax base amount notice (§ 35b(1) GewStG) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF114, Deadlines and filing |
| b0e37140e138:112 | \| Year of moving \| One return for the whole year, on the normal deadlines in de-income-tax. Foreign pay of the other period goes on Anlage N-AUS (de-employee-income-tax). Arrival and departure dates and foreign income must be declared \| | Exact wording retained at current LF115, Deadlines and filing |
| b0e37140e138:116 | - **Late partnership return (§ 152(6) and (7) AO).** The late-filing surcharge rules for tax returns apply. Amount: 0.0625% of the positive sum of the determined income for each started month late, at least EUR 25 a month, rounded down to whole euros, at most EUR 25,000 (§ 152(10) AO). It must be set if the return is not filed within 14 months after the end of the year (for 2026: by 29 February 2028), unless the deadline was extended (§ 152(2) and (3) no. 1 AO, applied by § 152(6)). The nil-tax let-outs of § 152(3) nos. 2 and 3 do not apply to this return. It is set first against the partnership if it has legal capacity (§ 152(4) sentence 3 AO). | Exact wording retained at current LF119, Penalties and interest |
| b0e37140e138:117 | - **Partner's own return.** Late filing, interest (0.15% a month from 1 April 2028) and late payment: see de-income-tax. | Exact wording retained at current LF120, Penalties and interest |
| b0e37140e138:118 | - **Interest is charged on the partner, not the partnership.** The determination sets no tax, so § 233a AO interest runs only on each partner's income tax. | Exact wording retained at current LF121, Penalties and interest |
| b0e37140e138:119 | - **Wrong figures.** Tax evasion (§ 370 AO) and reckless understatement (§ 378 AO): refer. | Exact wording retained at current LF122, Penalties and interest |
| b0e37140e138:123 | - **Foreign partner in a German partnership.** A non-resident partner of a German commercial partnership has German income from a permanent establishment (§ 49(1) no. 2 (a) EStG): de-non-resident-tax. The determination is still made (§ 180(3) no. 1 AO excludes it only where just one partner is liable in Germany). | Exact wording retained at current LF126, Cross-border points |
| b0e37140e138:124 | - **German partner in a foreign partnership.** Treaty-exempt profit is still determined where it matters for the partners' rate (§ 180(5) no. 1 AO) and raises the rate under § 32b(1) no. 3, with the EU/EEA exception for establishments that fail the active-business test. | Exact wording retained at current LF127, Cross-border points |
| b0e37140e138:125 | - **Leaving Germany with a partnership share.** A partner's share is business property. Leaving the country can be a deemed withdrawal or a trigger under leaving-germany-exit-tax, which also names a limited partner with more than 25% of the income as having a substantial interest. Refer before the move. | Exact wording retained at current LF128, Cross-border points |
| b0e37140e138:126 | - **Arrival with foreign pay or rent.** Use Part 2. Foreign tax paid on income of the other period gives no credit in Germany, because Germany does not tax that income; it only raises the rate. | Exact wording retained at current LF129, Cross-border points |
| b0e37140e138:127 | - **Social security.** Contributions linked to pay that a treaty exempts in Germany (the § 32b(1) no. 3 case) are not special expenses (§ 10(2) sentence 1 no. 1 EStG; BMF letter on Vorsorgeaufwendungen, Rn. 199), unless the pay was earned in an EU or EEA state or Switzerland and that state allows no deduction for them (§ 10(2) sentence 1 no. 1 (a) to (c)). Contributions on foreign pay of the months before arrival or after departure (the no. 2 case) are not settled here (see Not yet confirmed). Swiss AHV contributions do not reduce the amount added for the rate (H 32b). Social security itself: de-social-contributions. | Exact wording retained at current LF130, Cross-border points |
| b0e37140e138:133 | The Alpha GmbH & Co. KG runs a wholesale business in a town with a trade tax multiplier of 380%. Its general partner is a GmbH with no share in profit. Two limited partners: Anna (60%) and Ben (40%). In 2026 the KG's own profit, after booking Anna's management pay as a cost and before trade tax, is EUR 300,000. Anna's management pay from the KG: EUR 120,000 (a Sondervergütung). Anna pays EUR 4,000 interest on a loan she took to buy her share (a Sonderbetriebsausgabe). No trade tax add-backs or reductions. Anna is single, no church, no other income, and her deductible insurance is EUR 15,000. (Invented case; the multiplier is an input.) | Exact wording retained at current LF136, Example 1: managing limited partner of a GmbH & Co. KG |
| b0e37140e138:135 | - KG's trade income: 300,000 + 120,000 − 4,000 = EUR 416,000. Less EUR 24,500 = 391,500 × 3.5% = base amount EUR 13,702.50. Trade tax: × 380% = EUR 52,069.50. | Exact wording retained at current LF138, Example 1: managing limited partner of a GmbH & Co. KG |
| b0e37140e138:136 | - Anna's share: 60% × 300,000 + 120,000 − 4,000 = EUR 296,000. Ben's: EUR 120,000. | Exact wording retained at current LF139, Example 1: managing limited partner of a GmbH & Co. KG |
| b0e37140e138:137 | - Anna's share of the base amount by the general key (60.00%), the pay ignored: EUR 8,221.50. Of trade tax payable: EUR 31,241.70. | Exact wording retained at current LF140, Example 1: managing limited partner of a GmbH & Co. KG |
| b0e37140e138:138 | - Anna's taxable income: 296,000 − 15,000 − 36 = EUR 280,964. Zone 5: 0.45 × 280,964 − 19,470.38 = 106,963.42, so EUR 106,963. | Exact wording retained at current LF141, Example 1: managing limited partner of a GmbH & Co. KG |
| b0e37140e138:139 | - Credit: 4 × 8,221.50 = 32,886.00; tax on trade income = 106,963 (all her income is trade); trade tax share = 31,241.70. The lowest is EUR 31,241.70. EUR 1,644.30 of the four-times amount is lost because the multiplier is below 400%. | Exact wording retained at current LF142, Example 1: managing limited partner of a GmbH & Co. KG |
| b0e37140e138:140 | - Income tax: 106,963 − 31,241.70 = EUR 75,721.30. Surcharge: 5.5% = 4,164.67; the 11.9% cap (6,589.18) does not bite, so EUR 4,164.67. | Exact wording retained at current LF143, Example 1: managing limited partner of a GmbH & Co. KG |

| b0e37140e138:181 | Clara moves from London to Munich on 1 July 2026 and takes a job there. January to June she earned EUR 30,000 (converted) from her UK employer; she had no German income in those months. July to December she earns EUR 36,000 gross in Germany. Her Werbungskosten are below the lump sum. Deductible insurance for her German months, from the certificates: EUR 5,500. Single, no church, no other income. (Invented case.) | Exact wording retained at current LF184, Example 2: arrival on 1 July 2026 with foreign pay for January to June |
| b0e37140e138:183 | - Taxable income (resident for the whole year, § 2(7)): 36,000 − 1,230 − 5,500 − 36 = EUR 29,234. The basic allowance applies in full. | Exact wording retained at current LF186, Example 2: arrival on 1 July 2026 with foreign pay for January to June |
| b0e37140e138:184 | - The UK pay of January to June was not subject to German tax and raises the rate (§ 32b(1) no. 2). The lump sum was already used against the German pay, so nothing more comes off it (§ 32b(2) sentence 2 (a)): EUR 30,000 is added. | Exact wording retained at current LF187, Example 2: arrival on 1 July 2026 with foreign pay for January to June |
| b0e37140e138:185 | - Rate base: 29,234 + 30,000 = EUR 59,234. Zone 3: z = 4.1435; tax EUR 13,938. Special rate: 13,938 / 59,234 = 23.5304% (cut to four decimals). | Exact wording retained at current LF188, Example 2: arrival on 1 July 2026 with foreign pay for January to June |
| b0e37140e138:186 | - Income tax: 29,234 × 23.5304% = 6,878.88, rounded down EUR 6,878. Without the progression clause it would be EUR 4,002. Below EUR 20,350: no surcharge. | Exact wording retained at current LF189, Example 2: arrival on 1 July 2026 with foreign pay for January to June |
| b0e37140e138:187 | - Wage tax withheld July to December is credited against the EUR 6,878; the balance is due one month after the notice. | Exact wording retained at current LF190, Example 2: arrival on 1 July 2026 with foreign pay for January to June |

| 62b7639a5a68:18 | This skill covers two gaps the other Germany skills leave open. Part 1 is the partner in a partnership: how the partnership's profit is fixed once for all partners and then taxed in each partner's own return. Part 2 is the person who moves to or from Germany during 2026: one assessment as a resident for the whole year, with the foreign income of the other months raising the tax rate. | Exact wording retained at current LF18, Germany: partners' profit shares and the year of moving to or from Germany, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:20 | See also, and do not repeat here: | Exact wording retained at current LF20, Germany: partners' profit shares and the year of moving to or from Germany, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:22 | - **de-income-tax**: the 2026 tariff, special expenses, solidarity surcharge, church tax, the § 35 credit for a sole trader, deadlines and penalties for the partner's own return. | Exact wording retained at current LF22, Germany: partners' profit shares and the year of moving to or from Germany, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:23 | - **de-trade-tax**: trade tax at partnership level (add-backs, reductions, the EUR 24,500 allowance, multiplier), Abfärbung and gewerbliche Prägung in detail. | Exact wording retained at current LF23, Germany: partners' profit shares and the year of moving to or from Germany, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:24 | - **de-employee-income-tax**: wages, Werbungskosten, joint assessment, Anlage N-AUS. | Exact wording retained at current LF24, Germany: partners' profit shares and the year of moving to or from Germany, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:25 | - **de-non-resident-tax**: limited liability, German-source income (§ 49 EStG), the § 1(3) option, foreign tax credits (§ 34c EStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF25, Germany: partners' profit shares and the year of moving to or from Germany, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:26 | - **leaving-germany-exit-tax**: § 6 AStG on shares and the extended limited liability after a move. | Exact wording retained at current LF26, Germany: partners' profit shares and the year of moving to or from Germany, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:30 | **Part 1, partners.** | Exact wording retained at current LF30, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:32 | - **In scope.** A person resident in Germany (§ 1(1) EStG) who is a co-entrepreneur (Mitunternehmer) of a partnership: a partner in an OHG, a KG (general or limited partner), a GmbH & Co. KG, a GbR or a PartG that runs a business or a profession. Their income is "die Gewinnanteile der Gesellschafter ... und die Vergütungen, die der Gesellschafter von der Gesellschaft für seine Tätigkeit im Dienst der Gesellschaft oder für die Hingabe von Darlehen oder für die Überlassung von Wirtschaftsgütern bezogen hat" (§ 15(1) sentence 1 no. 2 EStG). A partner holding through another partnership is treated the same way (§ 15(1) sentence 1 no. 2 sentence 2). | Exact wording retained at current LF32, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:33 | - **Trade or profession.** A partnership whose partners all practise a free profession (§ 18(1) no. 1 EStG: doctors, lawyers, engineers, architects, tax advisers and the like) has income from self-employed work; § 18(4) applies § 15(1) no. 2 and § 15a to it, so the same profit share and Sondervergütung rules apply. It pays no trade tax unless one of two rules makes the whole activity a trade: | Exact wording retained at current LF33, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:34 |   - **Abfärbung (§ 15(3) no. 1 EStG).** Any commercial activity of its own, or commercial income from a share in another partnership, makes the whole activity commercial, whether that part makes a profit or a loss. | Exact wording retained at current LF34, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:35 |   - **Gewerbliche Prägung (§ 15(3) no. 2 EStG).** A partnership with no commercial activity whose only personally liable partners are capital companies, managed only by them or by non-partners. A GmbH & Co. KG run by its GmbH is the usual case. | Exact wording retained at current LF35, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:36 | - **Out of scope.** Partnerships that opted to be taxed as a company (§ 1a KStG); partners that are companies (de-corporate-tax); sale or closing of a partnership share (§§ 16, 34 EStG); a change of partners during the year; foreign partnerships. Refer. | Exact wording retained at current LF36, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:38 | **Part 2, the year of moving.** | Exact wording retained at current LF38, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:40 | - **In scope.** An individual who, in 2026, takes up or gives up a German home or habitual abode, so that they are fully liable for part of the year and either limited liable or not liable at all for the rest. Arrivals and departures alike. | Exact wording retained at current LF40, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:41 | - **The rule.** Income tax is an annual tax, assessed per calendar year (§ 2(7) sentences 1 and 2 EStG). "Besteht während eines Kalenderjahres sowohl unbeschränkte als auch beschränkte Einkommensteuerpflicht, so sind die während der beschränkten Einkommensteuerpflicht erzielten inländischen Einkünfte in eine Veranlagung zur unbeschränkten Einkommensteuerpflicht einzubeziehen" (§ 2(7) sentence 3). So there is one assessment as a resident, covering the whole year. | Exact wording retained at current LF41, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:42 | - **Out of scope.** The exit tax on shares when leaving (leaving-germany-exit-tax); a person who never becomes resident (de-non-resident-tax); the extended limited liability under § 2 AStG. | Exact wording retained at current LF42, Who this applies to |

| 62b7639a5a68:53 | \| Trade tax credit, partner's share \| 4.0 × the partner's share of the trade tax base amount, capped at the tax on trade income and at the partner's share of the trade tax actually payable \| 2026 \| https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/estg/__35.html (§ 35(1) no. 2, (1) sentence 5, (2), (4)) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF53, Rates, bands and thresholds |

| 62b7639a5a68:56 | \| Retained profits (§ 34a), rate on profit left in the business \| 28.25% for assessment years up to 2027; 27% for 2028 and 2029; 26% for 2030 and 2031; 25% from 2032 \| 2026 \| https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/estg/__34a.html (§ 34a(1)) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF56, Rates, bands and thresholds |

| 62b7639a5a68:66 | **Step 1: the partnership's profit is fixed once.** Where several people share in income, it is fixed separately and uniformly for all of them (§ 180(1) sentence 1 no. 2 (a) AO; § 179(2) AO). The determination notice (Feststellungsbescheid) binds each partner's own assessment. It fixes: | Exact wording retained at current LF66, Part 1: partners |
| 62b7639a5a68:68 | 1. **The partnership's own profit** (Gesamthandsgewinn), from its books or cash accounts. Trade tax is not deductible (§ 4(5b) EStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF68, Part 1: partners |
| 62b7639a5a68:69 | 2. **Each partner's Sondervergütungen**: pay for work for the partnership, interest on loans to it, rent for assets let to it (§ 15(1) no. 2 EStG). If the partnership booked them as costs, they are added back to that partner. They are business income, not wages, interest or rent. | Exact wording retained at current LF69, Part 1: partners |
| 62b7639a5a68:70 | 3. **Each partner's special business income and costs** (Sonderbetriebseinnahmen and -ausgaben): for example interest on a loan the partner took to buy the share, or the partner's own costs on a building let to the partnership. Each partner reports these to the partnership so they go into the determination. | Exact wording retained at current LF70, Part 1: partners |
| 62b7639a5a68:71 | 4. **The split.** Profit share by the partnership agreement, plus the partner's own items from 2 and 3. | Exact wording retained at current LF71, Part 1: partners |
| 62b7639a5a68:72 | 5. **Other items**: income exempt under a treaty that matters for the partners' rate (§ 180(5) no. 1 AO), and tax withheld to be credited (§ 180(5) no. 2 AO); for a commercial partnership, the § 35 figures (Step 3). | Exact wording retained at current LF72, Part 1: partners |
| 62b7639a5a68:74 | **Step 2: the partner's own return.** The partner takes the determined share into their own return as business income (trade or self-employed), adds any other income, and from there follows de-income-tax: special expenses, tariff, surcharge, church tax. The partner does not recompute the share; if it is wrong, the determination notice is what gets challenged. | Exact wording retained at current LF74, Part 1: partners |
| 62b7639a5a68:76 | **Step 3: trade tax and the § 35 credit.** A commercial partnership pays trade tax itself (§ 5 GewStG; see de-trade-tax). Its trade income starts from the profit fixed in Step 1, Sondervergütungen and special items included (§ 7 GewStG). A partner's share of profit from another partnership is taken out again (§ 9 no. 2 GewStG), because that partnership pays its own trade tax. The base amount, the trade tax actually payable and each partner's share of both are fixed in the same determination (§ 35(2) sentence 1 and (4) EStG). Each partner's share follows the general profit-sharing key, ignoring advance shares (§ 35(2) sentence 2). The BMF letter on § 35 says the same for Sondervergütungen and for results of special and supplementary balance sheets (BMF 3 November 2016, Rn. 22), and a partner that cannot use the credit, such as a GmbH general partner, still takes its key share (Rn. 24). A managing partner's Sondervergütung therefore gives no extra credit. The partner's credit is the lowest of: 4 × share of the base amount; the tax on trade income in the partner's own return (positive trade income / all positive income × tariff tax, § 35(1) sentence 2); the share of trade tax actually payable. Unused credit is lost. | Exact wording retained at current LF76, Part 1: partners |
| 62b7639a5a68:78 | **Step 4: losses of limited partners (§ 15a EStG).** A limited partner's loss share cannot be set against other income, or carried back or forward under § 10d, so far as it creates or increases a negative capital account. Exception: losses up to the amount by which the registered liability exceeds the contribution actually paid, if the partner is entered in the commercial register, the liability is proven, and a claim under it is not excluded by contract or unlikely (§ 15a(1) sentences 2 and 3). The blocked loss is only set against later profits from the same partnership (§ 15a(2)). It is fixed each year in a separate notice (§ 15a(4)). Withdrawals that create or increase a negative capital account are added as profit, up to the losses that were deductible in that year and the ten years before (§ 15a(3)). The same rule applies to atypical silent partners and to GbR partners whose liability is excluded or unlikely (§ 15a(5)). | Exact wording retained at current LF78, Part 1: partners |
| 62b7639a5a68:80 | **Step 5: the retained-profits option (§ 34a EStG), in summary.** A partner whose profit is computed from balance sheets (§ 4(1) or § 5) and whose share is over 10% or over EUR 10,000 may apply, per partnership share and per year, for profit left in the business to be taxed at 28.25% in 2026 instead of the tariff. Profit left in = profit less the positive balance of withdrawals over contributions, plus trade tax (§ 34a(2)). When later withdrawals exceed later profits, the amount fixed as liable to recapture is taxed at 25% (§ 34a(4)). The surcharge applies to both. The calculation is not set out here: refer. | Exact wording retained at current LF80, Part 1: partners |
| 62b7639a5a68:84 | **Step 1: split the year.** Fix the date the German home or habitual abode began or ended. The months before arrival or after departure are the "other period". | Exact wording retained at current LF84, Part 2: the year of moving |
| 62b7639a5a68:86 | **Step 2: the taxable base.** It contains: | Exact wording retained at current LF86, Part 2: the year of moving |
| 62b7639a5a68:88 | - all income of the resident months, worldwide (§ 2(1) EStG: income earned "während seiner unbeschränkten Einkommensteuerpflicht"); | Exact wording retained at current LF88, Part 2: the year of moving |
| 62b7639a5a68:89 | - German-source income of the other period (§ 49 EStG; see de-non-resident-tax), for example rent from a German flat let before arrival (§ 2(7) sentence 3); | Exact wording retained at current LF89, Part 2: the year of moving |
| 62b7639a5a68:90 | - the full basic allowance and the full employee lump sum: there is no cut by months for a resident assessment. Special expenses of the resident months are deducted as usual; for contributions paid in the other months see Cross-border points. | Exact wording retained at current LF90, Part 2: the year of moving |
| 62b7639a5a68:92 | **Step 3: income that raises the rate.** These are left out of the base but added to find the rate (§ 32b(1) sentence 1): | Exact wording retained at current LF92, Part 2: the year of moving |
| 62b7639a5a68:94 | - **No. 2: foreign income of the other period** that was not subject to German tax ("ausländische Einkünfte, die im Veranlagungszeitraum nicht der deutschen Einkommensteuer unterlegen haben"), for cases of part-year full liability, including § 2(7) sentence 3 cases. The official guidance confirms: on arrival or departure, income earned outside the period of full liability counts for the progression clause if it is not subject to limited liability (EStH 2025, H 32b "Zeitweise unbeschränkte Steuerpflicht"). | Exact wording retained at current LF94, Part 2: the year of moving |
| 62b7639a5a68:95 | - **No. 3: income a tax treaty exempts in Germany**, in the resident months (often foreign employment income or foreign rent). | Exact wording retained at current LF95, Part 2: the year of moving |
| 62b7639a5a68:97 | **Exception for EU and EEA items under no. 3 only (§ 32b(1) sentence 2).** Treaty-exempt rent from property in another EU or EEA state, profits of an EU or EEA farm, and profits of an EU or EEA commercial establishment that fails the active-business test of § 2a(2), do not raise the rate. The exception is written for no. 3 only. Foreign rent of the months before arrival falls under no. 2 and raises the rate wherever the property is. | Exact wording retained at current LF97, Part 2: the year of moving |
| 62b7639a5a68:99 | **Step 4: the amount added (§ 32b(2)).** Foreign income is computed under German rules: profit or surplus, not gross receipts. For foreign pay, the EUR 1,230 employee lump sum is deducted only to the extent it was not already deducted against German pay, and actual Werbungskosten only so far as all Werbungskosten together exceed the lump sum (§ 32b(2) sentence 2 (a) and (b)). Extraordinary income in it counts at one fifth. Foreign losses lower the rate and can bring it to zero (EStH 2025, H 32b "Ausländische Verluste"). Contributions to the Swiss AHV do not reduce the amount added (same, citing BFH 18 April 2012). | Exact wording retained at current LF99, Part 2: the year of moving |
| 62b7639a5a68:101 | **Step 5: the tax.** Tariff tax on (taxable income + amount added), divided by that sum, cut to four decimal places of a percent = special rate. Special rate × taxable income, rounded down = income tax. Then the surcharge and church tax on that tax (de-income-tax). | Exact wording retained at current LF101, Part 2: the year of moving |
| 62b7639a5a68:103 | **Joint assessment in the year of moving.** Spouses may choose it if both were fully liable and not permanently separated at some time in the year (§ 26(1) EStG). If both move together, the year qualifies. | Exact wording retained at current LF103, Part 2: the year of moving |
| 62b7639a5a68:107 | \| Step \| Rule \| | Exact wording retained at current LF107, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:109 | \| Partnership return \| Erklärung zur gesonderten und einheitlichen Feststellung (main form ESt 1 B, with Anlage FB for the partners and Anlage FE-G or FE-S for the income; names from the ELSTER 2025 guide). A partnership with legal capacity files it, and if it does not, each partner must; for one without legal capacity, each partner (§ 181(2) AO). Electronic only, unless the office allows paper for hardship (§ 181(2a) AO) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF109, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:110 | \| Partnership return deadline, own preparation \| Seven months after year end (§ 149(2) AO, applied by § 181(1) AO): 31 July 2027 is a Saturday, so Monday 2 August 2027 (§ 108(3) AO) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF110, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:111 | \| Partnership return deadline, tax adviser \| Last day of February of the second year after: 29 February 2028 (§ 149(3) no. 5 AO). The office can ask for it earlier (§ 149(4) AO) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF111, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:112 | \| Trade tax return of a commercial partnership \| Same deadlines (§ 149(3) no. 3 AO); see de-trade-tax \| | Exact wording retained at current LF112, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:113 | \| Partner's own return \| Deadlines in de-income-tax (2 August 2027, or 29 February 2028 with an adviser) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF113, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:114 | \| Changes flow through \| A change to the determination changes the partner's assessment; a change to profit changes the trade tax base amount notice (§ 35b(1) GewStG) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF114, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:115 | \| Year of moving \| One return for the whole year, on the normal deadlines in de-income-tax. Foreign pay of the other period goes on Anlage N-AUS (de-employee-income-tax). Arrival and departure dates and foreign income must be declared \| | Exact wording retained at current LF115, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:119 | - **Late partnership return (§ 152(6) and (7) AO).** The late-filing surcharge rules for tax returns apply. Amount: 0.0625% of the positive sum of the determined income for each started month late, at least EUR 25 a month, rounded down to whole euros, at most EUR 25,000 (§ 152(10) AO). It must be set if the return is not filed within 14 months after the end of the year (for 2026: by 29 February 2028), unless the deadline was extended (§ 152(2) and (3) no. 1 AO, applied by § 152(6)). The nil-tax let-outs of § 152(3) nos. 2 and 3 do not apply to this return. It is set first against the partnership if it has legal capacity (§ 152(4) sentence 3 AO). | Exact wording retained at current LF119, Penalties and interest |
| 62b7639a5a68:120 | - **Partner's own return.** Late filing, interest (0.15% a month from 1 April 2028) and late payment: see de-income-tax. | Exact wording retained at current LF120, Penalties and interest |
| 62b7639a5a68:121 | - **Interest is charged on the partner, not the partnership.** The determination sets no tax, so § 233a AO interest runs only on each partner's income tax. | Exact wording retained at current LF121, Penalties and interest |
| 62b7639a5a68:122 | - **Wrong figures.** Tax evasion (§ 370 AO) and reckless understatement (§ 378 AO): refer. | Exact wording retained at current LF122, Penalties and interest |
| 62b7639a5a68:126 | - **Foreign partner in a German partnership.** A non-resident partner of a German commercial partnership has German income from a permanent establishment (§ 49(1) no. 2 (a) EStG): de-non-resident-tax. The determination is still made (§ 180(3) no. 1 AO excludes it only where just one partner is liable in Germany). | Exact wording retained at current LF126, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:127 | - **German partner in a foreign partnership.** Treaty-exempt profit is still determined where it matters for the partners' rate (§ 180(5) no. 1 AO) and raises the rate under § 32b(1) no. 3, with the EU/EEA exception for establishments that fail the active-business test. | Exact wording retained at current LF127, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:128 | - **Leaving Germany with a partnership share.** A partner's share is business property. Leaving the country can be a deemed withdrawal or a trigger under leaving-germany-exit-tax, which also names a limited partner with more than 25% of the income as having a substantial interest. Refer before the move. | Exact wording retained at current LF128, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:129 | - **Arrival with foreign pay or rent.** Use Part 2. Foreign tax paid on income of the other period gives no credit in Germany, because Germany does not tax that income; it only raises the rate. | Exact wording retained at current LF129, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:130 | - **Social security.** Contributions linked to pay that a treaty exempts in Germany (the § 32b(1) no. 3 case) are not special expenses (§ 10(2) sentence 1 no. 1 EStG; BMF letter on Vorsorgeaufwendungen, Rn. 199), unless the pay was earned in an EU or EEA state or Switzerland and that state allows no deduction for them (§ 10(2) sentence 1 no. 1 (a) to (c)). Contributions on foreign pay of the months before arrival or after departure (the no. 2 case) are not settled here (see Not yet confirmed). Swiss AHV contributions do not reduce the amount added for the rate (H 32b). Social security itself: de-social-contributions. | Exact wording retained at current LF130, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:136 | The Alpha GmbH & Co. KG runs a wholesale business in a town with a trade tax multiplier of 380%. Its general partner is a GmbH with no share in profit. Two limited partners: Anna (60%) and Ben (40%). In 2026 the KG's own profit, after booking Anna's management pay as a cost and before trade tax, is EUR 300,000. Anna's management pay from the KG: EUR 120,000 (a Sondervergütung). Anna pays EUR 4,000 interest on a loan she took to buy her share (a Sonderbetriebsausgabe). No trade tax add-backs or reductions. Anna is single, no church, no other income, and her deductible insurance is EUR 15,000. (Invented case; the multiplier is an input.) | Exact wording retained at current LF136, Example 1: managing limited partner of a GmbH & Co. KG |
| 62b7639a5a68:138 | - KG's trade income: 300,000 + 120,000 − 4,000 = EUR 416,000. Less EUR 24,500 = 391,500 × 3.5% = base amount EUR 13,702.50. Trade tax: × 380% = EUR 52,069.50. | Exact wording retained at current LF138, Example 1: managing limited partner of a GmbH & Co. KG |
| 62b7639a5a68:139 | - Anna's share: 60% × 300,000 + 120,000 − 4,000 = EUR 296,000. Ben's: EUR 120,000. | Exact wording retained at current LF139, Example 1: managing limited partner of a GmbH & Co. KG |
| 62b7639a5a68:140 | - Anna's share of the base amount by the general key (60.00%), the pay ignored: EUR 8,221.50. Of trade tax payable: EUR 31,241.70. | Exact wording retained at current LF140, Example 1: managing limited partner of a GmbH & Co. KG |
| 62b7639a5a68:141 | - Anna's taxable income: 296,000 − 15,000 − 36 = EUR 280,964. Zone 5: 0.45 × 280,964 − 19,470.38 = 106,963.42, so EUR 106,963. | Exact wording retained at current LF141, Example 1: managing limited partner of a GmbH & Co. KG |
| 62b7639a5a68:142 | - Credit: 4 × 8,221.50 = 32,886.00; tax on trade income = 106,963 (all her income is trade); trade tax share = 31,241.70. The lowest is EUR 31,241.70. EUR 1,644.30 of the four-times amount is lost because the multiplier is below 400%. | Exact wording retained at current LF142, Example 1: managing limited partner of a GmbH & Co. KG |
| 62b7639a5a68:143 | - Income tax: 106,963 − 31,241.70 = EUR 75,721.30. Surcharge: 5.5% = 4,164.67; the 11.9% cap (6,589.18) does not bite, so EUR 4,164.67. | Exact wording retained at current LF143, Example 1: managing limited partner of a GmbH & Co. KG |

| 62b7639a5a68:184 | Clara moves from London to Munich on 1 July 2026 and takes a job there. January to June she earned EUR 30,000 (converted) from her UK employer; she had no German income in those months. July to December she earns EUR 36,000 gross in Germany. Her Werbungskosten are below the lump sum. Deductible insurance for her German months, from the certificates: EUR 5,500. Single, no church, no other income. (Invented case.) | Exact wording retained at current LF184, Example 2: arrival on 1 July 2026 with foreign pay for January to June |
| 62b7639a5a68:186 | - Taxable income (resident for the whole year, § 2(7)): 36,000 − 1,230 − 5,500 − 36 = EUR 29,234. The basic allowance applies in full. | Exact wording retained at current LF186, Example 2: arrival on 1 July 2026 with foreign pay for January to June |
| 62b7639a5a68:187 | - The UK pay of January to June was not subject to German tax and raises the rate (§ 32b(1) no. 2). The lump sum was already used against the German pay, so nothing more comes off it (§ 32b(2) sentence 2 (a)): EUR 30,000 is added. | Exact wording retained at current LF187, Example 2: arrival on 1 July 2026 with foreign pay for January to June |
| 62b7639a5a68:188 | - Rate base: 29,234 + 30,000 = EUR 59,234. Zone 3: z = 4.1435; tax EUR 13,938. Special rate: 13,938 / 59,234 = 23.5304% (cut to four decimals). | Exact wording retained at current LF188, Example 2: arrival on 1 July 2026 with foreign pay for January to June |
| 62b7639a5a68:189 | - Income tax: 29,234 × 23.5304% = 6,878.88, rounded down EUR 6,878. Without the progression clause it would be EUR 4,002. Below EUR 20,350: no surcharge. | Exact wording retained at current LF189, Example 2: arrival on 1 July 2026 with foreign pay for January to June |
| 62b7639a5a68:190 | - Wage tax withheld July to December is credited against the EUR 6,878; the balance is due one month after the notice. | Exact wording retained at current LF190, Example 2: arrival on 1 July 2026 with foreign pay for January to June |

| 62b7639a5a68:220 | 1. **Partners:** the partnership agreement's profit-sharing key, any advance shares, and every payment the partnership made to each partner (pay, interest, rent). This decides the share, the Sondervergütungen and the § 35 split. | Exact wording retained at current LF220, Ask the client first |
| 62b7639a5a68:221 | 2. **Partners:** what the partnership does and who manages it. A commercial side line or a GmbH as sole liable partner makes the whole partnership commercial and liable to trade tax. | Exact wording retained at current LF221, Ask the client first |
| 62b7639a5a68:222 | 3. **Limited partners with a loss:** the capital account at year end, the registered liability and the contribution actually paid (§ 15a). | Exact wording retained at current LF222, Ask the client first |
| 62b7639a5a68:223 | 4. **Year of moving:** the exact date the German home was taken up or given up, and where the person lived before or after. | Exact wording retained at current LF223, Ask the client first |
| 62b7639a5a68:224 | 5. **Year of moving:** all foreign income of the other months, by type and amount, and any foreign income in the resident months that a treaty exempts (and where the property or establishment is). | Exact wording retained at current LF224, Ask the client first |
| 62b7639a5a68:225 | 6. Married? Did the spouse move on the same date? | Exact wording retained at current LF225, Ask the client first |

## Writer checks and limits

The full current Guide and actual first-introduction/main bodies were read. Same-writer common tariff/deadline sources are reused; partnership and selected original own official2025 handbook/ELSTER clauses were actually read29October2026. The four-decimal percentage truncation is expressly the published2025 example inference, not a quoted statutory rounding clause or2026 software observation. All example inputs are fictional; exactFraction controls recompute both current oa cases, including after-credit Soli and the general profit-key allocation. The detailed34a election/recapture method, exact2026 determination forms and part-year foreign-period social-insurance deduction remain precisely stated referrals/evidence limits. No complete foreign treaty selection or limited-partner capital/debt ledger is invented. No independent or country PASS.
